I think one friend had given me money to hold (?) and I took some of it to buy some fruit pastilles. My friends realized what had happened and were angry with me. I made up some stupid excuse and offered to give the girl whose money I had taken, the fruit pastilles that I had just opened. I never did anything like that again as I felt so bad about it. I came from a poor family, and had very little, but that was no excuse for what I did. I suppose my friends forgave me eventually, but I'm not sure about that. I think one friend had given me money to hold (?) and I took some of it to buy some fruit pastilles. My friends realized what had happened and were angry with me. I made up some stupid excuse and offered to give the girl whose money I had taken, the fruit pastilles that I had just opened. I never did anything like that again as I felt so bad about it. I came from a poor family, and had very little, but that was no excuse for what I did. I suppose my friends forgave me eventually, but I'm not sure about that. I always pretty much felt like an outsider at school. I had one very good friend, but not a lot of other friends. I was very conscious of not having what they did. My father was an alcoholic and were had very little. I know that my clothes were shabby and that I didn't have much variety of clothing. I'm not sure if anything factored into what I did. I really don't think so. Maybe it was more a case of jealousy and wanting to have the same treats that the others were having. In spite of any of this it was inexcusable. I always pretty much felt like an outsider at school. I had one very good friend, but not a lot of other friends. I was very conscious of not having what they did. My father was an alcoholic and were had very little. I know that my clothes were shabby and that I didn't have much variety of clothing. I'm not sure if anything factored into what I did. I really don't think so. Maybe it was more a case of jealousy and wanting to have the same treats that the others were having. In spite of any of this it was inexcusable. I came from a poor family, and had very little, but that was no excuse for what I did. I suppose my friends forgave me eventually, but I'm not sure about that. I always pretty much felt like an outsider at school. I had one very good friend, but not a lot of other friends. I was very conscious of not having what they did. My father was an alcoholic and we had very little. I know that my clothes were shabby and that I didn't have much variety of clothing. I'm not sure if anything factored into what I did. I really don't think so. Maybe it was more a case of jealousy and wanting to have the same treats that the others were having. In spite of any of this it was inexcusable. I made a very big point of teaching my own children to be scrupulously honest. In fact, my 4 year old daughter once took a sweet from a store display, and I insisted that she apologize to the manager for taking it. She has her own grown children now, but she has never forgotten it. I made up some stupid excuse and offered to give the girl whose money I had taken, the fruit pastilles that I had just opened. I never did anything like that again as I felt so bad about it. I came from a poor family, and had very little, but that was no excuse for what I did. I suppose my friends forgave me eventually, but I'm not sure about that. I always pretty much felt like an outsider at school. I had one very good friend, but not a lot of other friends. I was very conscious of not having what they did. My father was an alcoholic and we had very little. I know that my clothes were shabby and that I didn't have much variety of clothing. I'm not sure if anything factored into what I did. I really don't think so. Maybe it was more a case of jealousy and wanting to have the same treats that the others were having. In spite of any of this it was inexcusable. I made a very big point of teaching my own children to be scrupulously honest. In fact, my 4 year old daughter once took a sweet from a store display, and I insisted that she apologize to the manager for taking it. I always pretty much felt like an outsider at school. I had one very good friend, but not a lot of other friends. I was very conscious of not having what they did. My father was an alcoholic and we had very little. I know that my clothes were shabby and that I didn't have much variety of clothing. I'm not sure if anything factored into what I did. I really don't think so. Maybe it was more a case of jealousy and wanting to have the same treats that the others were having. In spite of any of this it was inexcusable. I made a very big point of teaching my own children to be scrupulously honest. In fact, my 4 year old daughter once took a sweet from a store display, and I insisted that she apologize to the manager for taking it. She has her own grown children now, but she has never forgotten it. I hope that it was a positive experience for her and that she was able to pass along the concept of honesty to her children, and that they will also be able to do the same. Sharing important values affects families and society in general.
